@website/v4/src/pages/keybindings.astro @website/v4/src/data/keybindings.ts @website/v4/src/styles/tokens.css @website/v4/src/layouts/SiteLayout.astro @website/v4/src/styles/global.css

- I redesigned the Token editor website from v3 to v4 using Astro, located in website/v4/.
- I implemented an interactive keybindings viewer at website/v4/src/pages/keybindings.astro which is currently suspected to be broken.
- The viewer uses a 3-pane IDE-style layout inspired by the sema-lisp playground: a sidebar explorer, a YAML editor pane, and a detail inspector pane.
- Keybinding data is structured in website/v4/src/data/keybindings.ts and organized into categories (files, editing, selection, navigation, workspace).
- Interactivity is handled via vanilla JavaScript in a script tag within the Astro file, using data attributes and inline JSON to update the UI when sidebar items are clicked.
- The website uses a design system based on the Fleet Dark theme, with colors and typography defined in website/v4/src/styles/tokens.css.
- I already updated the palette from VS Code blue to Fleet's warm orange (#CC7832) and golden (#FFC66D) accents.
- The layout relies on CSS Grid (grid-template-columns: 200px 1fr 320px) and is designed to be responsive, stacking panes at smaller breakpoints.

i think the keybindings viewer is broken

## Assistant

Found it. Keys like `⌥+Click`, `Page Up`, `Page Down`, `Home`, `End`, `⌃Home`, `⌃End` are being split character-by-character, so "Page Up" becomes `P`, `a`, `g`, `e`, ` `, `U`, `p` as individual `<kbd>` elements. The `keys.split('')` logic doesn't handle multi-character key names.

The keys need a smarter split — treating symbols like `⌘⇧⌥⌃⎋⇥↩⌫⌦←→↑↓` as individual keys, and words like `Click`, `Home`, `End`, `Page Up`, `Page Down` as whole tokens.

## Assistant

The SSR-rendered keys look correct (`⌘` and `S` as separate `<kbd>` elements). The `${a}` in the script is the minified JS template literal — that's expected.

**The bug:** `keys.split('')` was splitting multi-character key names like `Page Up`, `Home`, `End`, `⌥+Click` into individual characters (e.g., `P` `a` `g` `e` ` ` `U` `p`), rendering each as a separate `<kbd>`.

**The fix:** Added a `splitKeys()` function (both in the Astro frontmatter and the client script) that uses a regex to tokenize keys properly — matching `Page Up`, `Page Down`, `Click`, `Home`, `End` as whole words, and modifier symbols (`⌘⇧⌥⌃` etc.) individually.

```
The CSV viewer in Token Editor is implemented as a specialized grid renderer within the unified `Renderer` struct. It provides a spreadsheet-like interface for viewing and editing CSV/TSV data, complete with row/column headers and cell navigation.

### Visual Style and Layout
The CSV viewer uses a classic spreadsheet layout:
- **Grid Layout**: A fixed-height header row (A, B, C...) and a fixed-width row number column (1, 2, 3...).
- **Cell Rendering**: Text is rendered within cells with 4px horizontal padding. Numbers are automatically detected and right-aligned, while text is left-aligned and truncated with an ellipsis (`…`) if it exceeds the column width.
- **Selection & Focus**: The selected cell is highlighted with a background color and a 2px thick border on all sides.
- **Cell Editing**: When editing, a `TextFieldRenderer` overlay is drawn directly over the cell, allowing for horizontal scrolling within the cell during text entry.
- **Styling**: Colors are driven by a dedicated `theme.csv` section, including specific colors for `grid_line`, `header_background`, `header_foreground`, and `selected_cell_background`.

### Relevant Files:
- [src/view/mod.rs#L1482-L1709](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L1482-L1709) - Main `render_csv_grid` implementation and data-to-pixel mapping.
- [src/view/mod.rs#L1713-L1781](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L1713-L1781) - Overlay implementation for the cell editor.
- [src/csv/render.rs#L48-L120](file://~/code/token-editor/src/csv/render.rs#L48-L120) - `CsvRenderLayout` logic for calculating column widths and grid positions.
- [src/csv/render.rs#L12-L46](file://~/code/token-editor/src/csv/render.rs#L12-L46) - Helpers for column labels (A, B, AA...), number detection, and text truncation.
- [src/csv/model.rs#L358-L450](file://~/code/token-editor/src/csv/model.rs#L358-L450) - `CsvState` and `CsvData` structures representing the spreadsheet state.
- [src/view/hit_test.rs#L521-L530](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/hit_test.rs#L521-L530) - Hit-testing logic to detect clicks on specific CSV cells.
- [src/update/csv.rs](file://~/code/token-editor/src/update/csv.rs) - Controller logic for CSV interactions (navigation, editing, toggling mode).
```

```
The Token editor has a dedicated CLI argument parsing system using `clap` and a separate headless rendering implementation used for performance profiling. While there is no user-facing `--screenshot` flag in the main binary yet, the `profile_render` tool provides the necessary infrastructure for off-screen rendering to a pixel buffer.

### CLI Argument Handling
The editor uses `clap` to parse arguments into a `CliArgs` struct, which is then converted into a `StartupConfig`.
- [src/cli.rs#L13-L35](file://~/code/token-editor/src/cli.rs#L13-L35) - `CliArgs` struct definition (supports opening paths, `--line`, `--column`, `--new`, and `--wait`).
- [src/main.rs#L29-L34](file://~/code/token-editor/src/main.rs#L29-L34) - Main entry point parsing arguments and initializing the `App`.

### Headless & Capture Functionality
There is a specialized headless rendering binary used for profiling that renders the editor state into a memory buffer without a window.
- [src/bin/profile_render.rs#L90-L159](file://~/code/token-editor/src/bin/profile_render.rs#L90-L159) - Implements a headless render loop that draws the `AppModel` into a `Vec<u32>` buffer using `fontdue`.
- [src/view/frame.rs#L34-L125](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/frame.rs#L34-L125) - The `Frame` abstraction allows drawing to any `&mut [u32]` buffer, making it easy to redirect output to an image file.
- [src/view/mod.rs#L66-L80](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L66-L80) - The main `Renderer` maintains a `back_buffer: Vec<u32>` which could be captured for screenshots.

### Screenshot Infrastructure
The project contains several screenshots in the `assets/` directory (e.g., `screenshot-v2-pretty.png`), and the `image` crate (with `png` support) is already a dependency in `Cargo.toml`.
- [Cargo.toml#L51](file://~/code/token-editor/Cargo.toml#L51) - `image` crate dependency.
- [src/bin/profile_render.rs#L44-L49](file://~/code/token-editor/src/bin/profile_render.rs#L44-L49) - Configurable window dimensions for headless rendering.
```

## 1) Homepage visual improvements (website/v4/src/pages/index.astro)

### TL;DR
The page is already clean and on-brand, but it currently *undersells* the product by relying on a “fake editor” hero and a single static screenshot later. Make the hero feel like a real product shot, add one “proof” section (performance + credibility), and tighten micro-interactions and hierarchy to increase perceived quality.

### What’s weak / missing right now
- **Hero credibility gap:** the hero “editor” is HTML art, not the real app. It looks nice, but visitors immediately ask “is that real?”
- **No “proof” section:** you claim *Absurdly fast* and show stats, but there’s no concrete benchmark, gif/video, or “how it compares” nugget.
- **Single screenshot buried:** the real screenshot is below a languages marquee; many users won’t scroll that far.
- **Lack of narrative flow:** Stats → Features → Languages → Screenshot is a bit disjointed. The screenshot should support the claims earlier.
- **Micro-interactions are uneven:** install copy has a nice touch; other elements (stats, tabs, cards) could feel more alive.

### Concrete improvements (simple path)
1) **Replace/upgrade the hero visual**
   - **Use an actual app screenshot** (auto-generated, see plan below) as the primary hero visual.
   - Keep the current HTML “fake editor” as:
     - a fallback while loading, or
     - a small secondary “callout” card that demonstrates multi-cursor with animated cursors.
   - Add a “window chrome” treatment (macOS traffic lights) to make it instantly recognizable as a native app.

2) **Move the screenshot *into* the hero area**
   - Current hero is a 2-column grid; make the right column a **big screenshot card** with:
     - subtle border (`--border-strong`)
     - slightly stronger shadow (`--shadow-lg`)
     - a soft accent glow (`--shadow-glow`) on hover only
   - Add a small caption under it: “Fleet Dark theme · Rust file · 3 splits · CSV view”

3) **Add one “Performance / No-BS” proof section (high-impact)**
   - Immediately after Stats, add a short section with 2 columns:
     - Left: “Why it’s fast” bullets (CPU renderer, no Electron, incremental parsing, etc.)
     - Right: tiny benchmark card (even if rough):
       “Startup: <50ms · 60fps typical · 15–25MB RAM (idle)”
       (If you don’t want numbers yet, show “Measured on M2 / Linux / Windows” and link to a profiling page.)
   - Add a link: “See profiling setup →” (even if it’s a README).

4) **Tighten section hierarchy + reduce “marquee noise”**
   - The **Languages marquee** is visually fun, but it reads like filler unless it supports a claim.
   - Either:
     - move it *below* the Showcase screenshot, or
     - shrink it and pair it with a sentence: “Tree-sitter based highlighting for 20+ languages.”

5) **Make feature cards feel more “product-grade”**
   - Add consistent hover/focus states:
     - card border shifts to `--border-hover`
     - slight translateY(-2px)
     - subtle glow only on the “wide” feature card
   - Improve “Split views” demo: currently it’s abstract. Replace with a cropped real screenshot of a split layout (generated) instead of empty `.split-lines`.

6) **Micro-interactions polish (low effort, big perceived quality)**
   - **Hero CTA arrow:** animate the down arrow on hover (translateY + opacity) using `--duration-normal`/`--ease-out`.
   - **Tabs:** animate active tab underline (scaleX transition).
   - **Stats:** animate count-up once on first viewport entry (tiny JS IntersectionObserver).
   - **Copy button:** you already show ✓; add a subtle background flash (`--bg-active`) for 150ms.

7) **Add “social proof” without inventing logos**
   - Add a small “Open source / MIT / Built with AI (Amp threads)” trust row near the hero CTAs:
     - GitHub stars count (live fetch optional; or omit if you want no client JS)
     - “MIT Licensed”
     - “No telemetry” (if true)

### Rationale and trade-offs
- The biggest conversion lever for dev tools is **“show me the real thing”**. Real screenshots + one proof section will outperform additional feature copy.
- Avoid adding video until the screenshot pipeline exists. Static images + tasteful motion beats a half-baked demo.

### Risks / guardrails
- Don’t over-animate. Keep motion to hover/entry transitions and respect `prefers-reduced-motion`.
- Don’t add hard performance numbers unless you can reproduce them—link to methodology if you do.

### Effort
- Visual/layout tweaks: **M (1–3h)**
- Replacing hero with real screenshot + polishing: **M–L (3–6h)** depending on assets


---

## 2) Automated screenshot generation plan (Rust app → PNG → website)

### TL;DR
Create a deterministic, headless `token screenshot` subcommand (or `src/bin/screenshot.rs`) that loads a “scenario” (files + split layout + theme + window size), renders 1–3 warmup frames into the existing `Vec<u32>` back buffer via the `Frame` abstraction, then writes a PNG via the `image` crate. Wire it to a `make screenshots` target and a CI job.

---

### Recommended approach (simple path)

#### A) Add a `screenshot` binary or clap subcommand
**Prefer a separate binary at first** (`src/bin/screenshot.rs`) to avoid entangling main app lifecycle. You can later fold into `token screenshot` once stable.

**CLI sketch:**
```bash
cargo run --bin screenshot -- \
  --scenario screenshots/scenarios/hero.yaml \
  --out website/v4/public/screenshot-hero.png
```

Support flags (override scenario):
- `--theme themes/fleet-dark.yaml`
- `--width 2880 --height 1800` (retina)
- `--scale 2` (optional convenience; multiplies width/height)
- `--out-dir website/v4/public/`
- `--name screenshot-hero` (or derived from scenario)
- `--warmup-frames 2`

#### B) Define “scenarios” as small YAML files (deterministic)
Create: `screenshots/scenarios/*.yaml`

Example schema (keep minimal):
```yaml
name: hero
size: { width: 2880, height: 1800 }   # retina
theme: themes/fleet-dark.yaml
layout:
  splits:
    - file: samples/main.rs
    - file: samples/config.yaml
    - file: samples/data.csv
  direction: vertical
  sidebar: true
state:
  active_split: 0
  cursors:
    - split: 0
      positions: [{ line: 42, col: 12 }, { line: 48, col: 12 }]
  scroll:
    - split: 0
      top_line: 30
```

Why YAML scenarios:
- Works well with your existing YAML theme approach.
- Easy to add new shots without recompiling code logic.
- CI can iterate `screenshots/scenarios/*.yaml`.

#### C) Reuse `profile_render` machinery by extracting reusable headless render helpers
Right now `profile_render.rs` contains working headless rendering logic, but it’s tightly coupled to profiling and manual drawing loops.

Minimal refactor:
1. Extract into library module(s), e.g.:
   - `src/headless/mod.rs`
   - `src/headless/scenario.rs`
   - `src/headless/render.rs`

2. Reuse:
- Model creation logic (opening files / synthetic content)
- Layout computation (splits)
- `Frame` drawing path (preferred for long-term)
- Theme loading (YAML theme system)

3. For screenshot correctness, use the **same rendering code path** as the real app *as much as possible*:
- If your production renderer already can draw into `Renderer.back_buffer: Vec<u32>`, expose a “render one frame into provided buffer” entrypoint.
- If not yet possible, the “profile_render style” renderer is acceptable as a first iteration, but expect visual drift vs. real UI.

#### D) Implement “open files in splits” deterministically
Scenarios should be able to:
- open N files
- set split arrangement (vertical/horizontal grid)
- optionally open a CSV in “table mode” split

Implementation plan:
- Create the AppModel similarly to `create_model()` in `profile_render`.
- For each split:
  - create/load `Document` from disk (use `samples/` or explicit paths)
  - attach to an editor state
- Apply editor state:
  - `viewport.top_line`
  - cursor positions (multi-cursor)
  - active split index
- Apply UI state:
  - sidebar visible (if supported)
  - active tab (if supported)

If the current model/update system is message-driven (`update(Msg, ...)`), prefer sending the same messages the UI uses (less bespoke state mutation).

#### E) Render to PNG via `image`
Your buffers are `u32` in ARGB (0xAARRGGBB). `image` commonly uses RGBA8.

Conversion:
- iterate pixels:
  - extract A,R,G,B
  - write `[R,G,B,A]` into an `ImageBuffer<Rgba<u8>, Vec<u8>>`
- Save with `image::DynamicImage::ImageRgba8(img).save(path)?;`

Guardrail: confirm channel order once with a known solid color test.

#### F) Make it CI-friendly + stable
Determinism checklist:
- **Disable time-based blinking cursor** (render cursor “on” always in screenshot mode).
- Ensure font selection is stable in CI:
  - either embed a font file used by Token for screenshots, or
  - vendor a known font in repo and load it explicitly for screenshots.
- Avoid reading OS theme/fonts.
- Use warmup frames (`--warmup-frames 2`) so caches settle.

---

### “Interesting configurations” that make great editor screenshots
Aim for 3–6 canonical shots that map to homepage sections:

1) **Hero (flagship)**
- 3 splits: Rust + YAML config + CSV table mode
- sidebar visible (workspace tree)
- multi-cursor visible in Rust split
- command palette hint or subtle overlay (optional)

2) **Multi-cursor close-up**
- Single file, zoomed / larger font
- 4–8 cursors on repeated identifiers, selection highlights

3) **Split views**
- Same file in 2 splits at different scroll positions
- shows independent viewports

4) **CSV spreadsheet mode**
- A clean table with a selected cell, row highlight, numeric alignment

5) **Theme showcase (optional)**
- Fleet Dark (default) + 1 alternative theme
- Same content, same layout (easy A/B)

6) **“Minimal / distraction-free”**
- Single file, no sidebar, just code + status bar

---

### Wiring into the website + Make/CI
**Output targets:**
- `website/v4/public/screenshot-hero.png`
- `website/v4/public/screenshot-splits.png`
- etc.

**Make target (example):**
```make
.PHONY: screenshots
screenshots:
	cargo run --bin screenshot -- --all --out-dir website/v4/public
```

**CI job:**
- Run on push to main (or release tags)
- Upload artifacts or commit via a bot (your preference)
- Optional: compare PNG hashes to detect changes

---

### Risks and guardrails
- **Risk: screenshot renderer diverges from real renderer.**
  Guardrail: route screenshots through the same renderer APIs used by the GUI if at all possible; avoid a “separate implementation” long term.
- **Risk: CI font differences change output.**
  Guardrail: explicit font loading for screenshot mode.
- **Risk: flaky due to nondeterministic layout sizing.**
  Guardrail: scenario sets exact width/height, fixed scale, fixed UI toggles.

---

### When to consider the advanced path
Consider a more elaborate pipeline only if:
- you need animated gifs/videos,
- you need per-platform screenshots (Windows/macOS titlebars),
- you want pixel-perfect regression tests across renderer changes.

### Optional advanced path (outline)
- Add a `--record` mode that renders a short sequence and outputs APNG/GIF (still CPU-rendered).
- Add golden-image tests: `cargo test --features screenshot-tests` that renders and compares against checked-in PNGs with small tolerance.

---

### Effort
- First working screenshot binary (single scenario → PNG): **M (3–6h)**
- Scenario system + multiple shots + CI integration: **L (1–2d)**
```

scenario.theme {\n        // Try as file path first, then as builtin id\n        let theme_path = std::path::Path::new(theme_id);\n        if theme_path.exists() {\n            token::theme::from_file(theme_path)\n                .map_err(|e| anyhow::anyhow!(\"Theme error: {}\", e))?\n        } else {\n            token::theme::load_theme(theme_id)\n                .map_err(|e| anyhow::anyhow!(\"Theme error: {}\", e))?\n        }\n    } else {\n        Theme::default()\n    };\n\n    let mut model = AppModel {\n        editor_area,\n        ui: UiState::new(),\n        theme,\n        config: EditorConfig::default(),\n        window_size: (scenario.width, scenario.height),\n        line_height,\n        char_width,\n        metrics: token::model::ScaledMetrics::default(),\n        workspace: None,\n        dock_layout: token::panel::DockLayout::default(),\n        #[cfg(debug_assertions)]\n        debug_overlay: None,\n    };\n\n    // Add additional splits for remaining files\n    for file_spec in scenario.files.iter().skip(1) {\n        let direction = match scenario.split_direction {\n            SplitDir::Horizontal => token::model::editor_area::SplitDirection::Horizontal,\n            SplitDir::Vertical => token::model::editor_area::SplitDirection::Vertical,\n        };\n        update(&mut model, Msg::Layout(LayoutMsg::SplitFocused(direction)));\n\n        let file_content = std::fs::read_to_string(&file_spec.path)?;\n        if let Some(doc) = model.editor_area.focused_document_mut() {\n            doc.buffer = ropey::Rope::from_str(&file_content);\n        }\n\n        // Apply cursor/scroll\n        if let Some(editor) = model.editor_area.focused_editor_mut() {\n            if let Some(line) = file_spec.scroll_to {\n                editor.viewport.top_line = line;\n            }\n            if let Some(line) = file_spec.cursor_line {\n                if let Some(cursor) = editor.cursors.first_mut() {\n                    cursor.line = line;\n                    cursor.column = file_spec.cursor_column.unwrap_or(0);\n                }\n            }\n            // Add extra cursors\n            for pos in &file_spec.extra_cursors {\n                editor.cursors.push(token::model::cursor::Cursor::new(pos.line, pos.column));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n TextPainter, Renderer};\n    use token::model::editor_area::Rect;\n\n    let font = fontdue::Font::from_bytes(\n        include_bytes!(\"../../assets/JetBrainsMono.ttf\") as &[u8],\n        fontdue::FontSettings::default(),\n    ).expect(\"Font load failed\");\n\n    let scale = 2.0f32;\n    let font_size = 14.0 * scale;\n    let line_metrics = font.horizontal_line_metrics(font_size).unwrap();\n    let line_height = line_metrics.new_line_size.ceil() as usize;\n    let ascent = line_metrics.ascent;\n    let (m, _) = font.rasterize('M', font_size);\n    let char_width = m.advance_width;\n\n    let status_bar_height = line_height;\n    let sidebar_width = 0.0f32; // No sidebar for now\n\n    let mut buffer = vec![0u32; width * height];\n    let mut glyph_cache = std::collections::HashMap::new();\n\n    // Clear with background\n    let bg_color = model.theme.editor.background.to_argb_u32();\n    buffer.fill(bg_color);\n\n    // Compute layout\n    let available_rect = Rect::new(\n        sidebar_width,\n        0.0,\n        (width as f32) - sidebar_width,\n        (height as usize).saturating_sub(status_bar_height) as f32,\n    );\n    let splitters = model.editor_area\n        .compute_layout_scaled(available_rect, model.metrics.splitter_width);\n\n    // Render editor area\n    {\n        let mut frame = Frame::new(&mut buffer, width, height);\n        let mut painter = TextPainter::new(\n            &font, &mut glyph_cache, font_size, ascent, char_width, line_height,\n        );\n        Renderer::render_editor_area(&mut frame, &mut painter, model, &splitters);\n    }\n\n    // Render status bar\n    {\n        let mut frame = Frame::new(&mut buffer, width, height);\n        let mut painter = TextPainter::new(\n            &font, &mut glyph_cache, font_size, ascent, char_width, line_height,\n        );\n        Renderer::render_status_bar(&mut frame, &mut painter, model, width, height);\n    }\n\n    buffer\n}\n```\n\n**Step 3: Implement ARGB-to-RGBA PNG writer**\n\n```rust\nfn save_png(buffer: &[u32], width: u32, height: u32, path: &Path) -> Result<()> {\n    let mut rgba_buf: Vec<u8> = Vec::with_capacity((width * height * 4) as usize);\n    for &pixel in buffer {\n        // Buffer is ARGB (0xAARRGGBB) → need RGBA\n        let r = ((pixel >> 16) & 0xFF) as u8;\n        let g = ((pixel >> 8) & 0xFF) as u8;\n        let b = (pixel & 0xFF) as u8;\n        let a = ((pixel >> 24) & 0xFF) as u8;\n        rgba_buf.extend_from_slice(&[r, g, b, a]);\n    }\n\n    let img: image::ImageBuffer<image::Rgba<u8>, Vec<u8>> =\n        image::ImageBuffer::from_raw(width, height, rgba_buf)\n            .ok_or_else(|| anyhow::anyhow!(\"Failed to create image buffer\"))?;\n    img.save(path)?;\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n```\n\n**Step 4: Wire up main()**\n\n```rust\nfn main() -> Result<()> {\n    let args = Args::parse();\n\n    std::fs::create_dir_all(&args.out_dir)?;\n\n    let scenario.name, width, height);\n\n        let mut model = create_model_from_scenario(scenario)?;\n        model.window_size = (width, height);\n\n        let buffer = render_to_buffer(&mut model, width as usize, height as usize);\n\n        let out_path = args.out_dir.join(format!(\"screenshot-{}.png\", scenario.name));\n        save_png(&buffer, width, height, &out_path)?;\n        eprintln!(\"  → {}\", out_path.display());\n    }\n\n    eprintln!(\"Done! {} screenshot(s) generated.\", scenarios.len());\n    Ok(())\n}\n```\n\n**Step 5: Run build**\n\nRun: `cargo build --bin session (no handoff)\n- Continuous progress (no waiting)\n- Review checkpoints automatic\n\n**Efficiency gains:**\n- No file reading overhead (controller provides full text)\n- Controller curates exactly what context is needed\n- Subagent gets complete information upfront\n- Questions surfaced before work begins (not after)\n\n**Quality gates:**\n- Self-review catches issues before handoff\n- Two-stage review: spec compliance, then code quality\n- Review loops ensure fixes actually work\n- Spec compliance prevents over/under-building\n- Code quality ensures implementation is well-built\n\n**Cost:**\n- More subagent invocations (implementer + 2 reviewers per task)\n- Controller does more prep work (extracting all tasks upfront)\n- Review loops add iterations\n- But catches issues early (cheaper than debugging later)\n\n## Red Flags\n\n**Never:**\n- Start implementation on main/master branch without explicit user consent\n- Skip reviews (spec compliance OR code quality)\n- Proceed with unfixed issues\n- Dispatch multiple implementation subagents in parallel (conflicts)\n- Make subagent read plan file (provide full text instead)\n- Skip scene-setting context (subagent needs to understand where task fits)\n- Ignore subagent questions (answer before letting them proceed)\n- Accept \"close enough\" on spec compliance (spec reviewer found issues = not done)\n- Skip review loops (reviewer found issues = implementer fixes = review again)\n- Let implementer self-review replace actual review (both are needed)\n- **Start code quality review before spec compliance is ✅** (wrong order)\n- Move to next task while either review has open issues\n\n**If subagent asks questions:**\n- Answer clearly and completely\n- Provide additional context if needed\n- Don't rush them into implementation\n\n**If reviewer finds issues:**\n- Implementer (same subagent) fixes them\n- Reviewer reviews again\n- Repeat until approved\n- Don't skip the re-review\n\n**If subagent fails task:**\n- Dispatch fix subagent with specific instructions\n- Don't try to fix manually (context pollution)\n\n## Integration\n\n**Required workflow skills:**\n- use:**\n- **superpowers:test-driven-development** - Subagents follow TDD for each task\n\n**Alternative workflow:**\n- **superpowers:executing-plans** - Use for parallel session instead of
